budget, but a smaller representation is useful only if it preserves values well enough for the declared task. Inspect both the values and the bytes rather than selecting a dtype from its name alone.", "fact_ids": []}, {"id": "tb2", "purpose": "mechanism", "title": "The working rule", "content": "A numerical dtype specifies a representable value range and a bit width, so candidate integer and floating types need not preserve the same input values. Inspect a.dtype and explicitly cast with a.astype(candidate_dtype) when comparing representations. For a fixed-width numerical array, a.size × a.itemsize is its element-storage byte count, exposed by a.nbytes; it excludes non-element object overhead.", "fact_ids": ["f1", "f2", "f3"]}, {"id": "tb3", "purpose": "mechanism", "title": "Apply and verify", "content": "Compare the cast values with the reference values using arithmetic differences before accepting a lower-precision representation; casting can overflow or lose precision.", "fact_ids": ["f4"]}, {"id": "tb4", "purpose": "example", "title": "A bounded work example", "content": "Suppose a reference fixture contains six values and you are comparing fixed-width representations with element widths of eight and four bytes. Their element-storage totals are 6 × 8 = 48 bytes and 6 × 4 = 24 bytes. That comparison says nothing about other object overhead. Explicitly cast the reference into the candidate representation, inspect the candidate dtype and subtract reference values to reveal changes. For instance, if a supplied candidate represents a reference value 1.25 as 1.0, the difference is −0.25. Whether that is acceptable depends on the fixture's declared tolerance; the byte saving does not decide it. Keep the reference values available while checking the candidate, so the comparison is not between two copies of the already altered values.\n\nInspect a value near a declared precision or range boundary as well as ordinary values. The purpose is to expose the candidate conversion's actual difference from the retained reference, not to assume that every smaller dtype behaves badly or that every successful cast is acceptable.
